```python
# utils/file_utils.py
"""
VisionSpace AI — Production File Utilities
==========================================
Provides robust file I/O with:
  - Atomic JSON read / write
  - SHA-256 / MD5 checksum generation and verification
  - Lightweight version-controlled JSON store (append-only log + HEAD pointer)
  - Directory helpers (ensure, clean, size)
  - Output path builder aligned with config.OUTPUT_DIR structure
  - Kaggle-safe path resolution (works when /kaggle/working is cwd)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import shutil
import tempfile
import time
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import config
from utils.constants import JSON_INDENT, CHECKSUM_ALGORITHM, OUTPUT_SUBDIRS

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Path Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def resolve_output_path(subdir: str, filename: str) -> Path:
    """Return an absolute path inside config.OUTPUT_DIR / subdir / filename.

    Creates all intermediate directories automatically.  Works on both local
    Windows dev environments and Kaggle (/kaggle/working) without any change.

    Args:
        subdir:   One of the OUTPUT_SUBDIRS (e.g. "reports", "budgets").
        filename: Target filename including extension.

    Returns:
        Fully resolved :class:`pathlib.Path`.

    Example::

        path = resolve_output_path("reports", "session_001.json")
        # → <cwd>/outputs/reports/session_001.json
    """
    base = Path(config.OUTPUT_DIR).resolve()
    target = base / subdir / filename
    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    return target


def ensure_output_dirs() -> None:
    """Create all standard output sub-directories defined in ``OUTPUT_SUBDIRS``.

    Safe to call multiple times; silently skips existing directories.
    """
    base = Path(config.OUTPUT_DIR).resolve()
    for sub in OUTPUT_SUBDIRS:
        (base / sub).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    logger.debug("Output directory tree verified at: %s", base)


def directory_size_mb(path: str | Path) -> float:
    """Return the total size of a directory tree in megabytes.

    Args:
        path: Root directory to measure.

    Returns:
        Total size in MB, or 0.0 if path does not exist.
    """
    root = Path(path)
    if not root.exists():
        return 0.0
    total = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in root.rglob("*") if f.is_file())
    return round(total / (1024 ** 2), 3)


def clean_directory(path: str | Path, keep_structure: bool = True) -> int:
    """Remove all files inside *path* without deleting the directory itself.

    Args:
        path:           Directory to clean.
        keep_structure: If True, sub-directories are kept but emptied.
                        If False, all contents including sub-dirs are removed.

    Returns:
        Number of files deleted.
    """
    root = Path(path)
    if not root.exists():
        return 0

    deleted = 0
    for item in root.rglob("*"):
        if item.is_file():
            item.unlink()
            deleted += 1
        elif item.is_dir() and not keep_structure:
            shutil.rmtree(item, ignore_errors=True)

    logger.info("Cleaned %d files from %s", deleted, root)
    return deleted


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Atomic JSON I/O
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def save_json(data: Any, file_path: str | Path, *, indent: int = JSON_INDENT) -> None:
    """Atomically write *data* to *file_path* as JSON.

    Uses a temporary file + rename for crash-safe writes on all platforms.
    Parent directories are created automatically.

    Args:
        data:      Any JSON-serialisable Python object.
        file_path: Destination file path.
        indent:    JSON indentation level (default 4).

    Raises:
        TypeError: If *data* contains non-serialisable types.
    """
    target = Path(file_path)
    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    tmp_fd, tmp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(
        dir=target.parent, suffix=".tmp", prefix=".write_"
    )
    try:
        with os.fdopen(tmp_fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump(data, f, indent=indent, ensure_ascii=False)
        # Atomic rename — on POSIX this is O_RENAME; on Windows it overwrites
        os.replace(tmp_path, target)
    except Exception:
        # Clean up orphan temp file on error
        if os.path.exists(tmp_path):
            os.unlink(tmp_path)
        raise

    logger.debug("Saved JSON → %s (%d bytes)", target, target.stat().st_size)


def load_json(file_path: str | Path) -> Any:
    """Load and return the contents of a JSON file.

    Args:
        file_path: Source file path.

    Returns:
        Deserialised Python object.

    Raises:
        FileNotFoundError: If *file_path* does not exist.
        json.JSONDecodeError: If the file is not valid JSON.
    """
    target = Path(file_path)
    with open(target, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)


def load_json_safe(file_path: str | Path, default: Any = None) -> Any:
    """Like :func:`load_json` but returns *default* on any error.

    Useful in initialisation paths where a missing/corrupt file is acceptable.
    """
    try:
        return load_json(file_path)
    except Exception as exc:
        logger.warning("load_json_safe: could not load %s — %s", file_path, exc)
        return default


def append_json_lines(records: list[dict], file_path: str | Path) -> None:
    """Append *records* to a JSON-Lines file (one JSON object per line).

    Creates the file if it does not exist.  Useful for streaming log outputs.

    Args:
        records:   List of dicts to append.
        file_path: Target ``.jsonl`` file.
    """
    target = Path(file_path)
    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(target, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for rec in records:
            f.write(json.dumps(rec, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Checksum Utilities
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_checksum(
    file_path: str | Path,
    algorithm: str = CHECKSUM_ALGORITHM,
    chunk_size: int = 1 << 20,   # 1 MB
) -> str:
    """Compute and return the hex-digest checksum of a file.

    Args:
        file_path:  Path to the file.
        algorithm:  Hash algorithm name understood by :mod:`hashlib`
                    (e.g. ``"sha256"``, ``"md5"``).
        chunk_size: Read chunk size in bytes (default 1 MB).

    Returns:
        Lowercase hex-digest string.

    Raises:
        FileNotFoundError: If *file_path* does not exist.
        ValueError:        If *algorithm* is not supported.
    """
    try:
        h = hashlib.new(algorithm)
    except ValueError as exc:
        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported hash algorithm: '{algorithm}'") from exc

    with open(file_path, "rb") as f:
        while chunk := f.read(chunk_size):
            h.update(chunk)

    return h.hexdigest()


def verify_checksum(
    file_path: str | Path,
    expected: str,
    algorithm: str = CHECKSUM_ALGORITHM,
) -> bool:
    """Return True if the file's checksum matches *expected*.

    Args:
        file_path: Path to the file to verify.
        expected:  Expected hex-digest string (case-insensitive).
        algorithm: Hash algorithm used when producing *expected*.

    Returns:
        ``True`` if checksums match, ``False`` otherwise.
    """
    actual = compute_checksum(file_path, algorithm=algorithm)
    match = actual.lower() == expected.lower()
    if not match:
        logger.warning(
            "Checksum mismatch for %s: expected=%s actual=%s",
            file_path, expected[:12] + "…", actual[:12] + "…",
        )
    return match


def save_json_with_checksum(
    data: Any,
    file_path: str | Path,
) -> str:
    """Save *data* as JSON and return its SHA-256 checksum.

    Useful for verifying integrity of exported scene snapshots.

    Returns:
        Hex-digest SHA-256 string of the written file.
    """
    save_json(data, file_path)
    return compute_checksum(file_path)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Version-Controlled JSON Store
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class VersionedJSONStore:
    """Lightweight version-controlled store for a single JSON document.

    Layout on disk::

        <root>/
          HEAD               — plain-text: version number of current HEAD
          v0001.json         — first committed version
          v0002.json         — second committed version
          ...

    This is intentionally simple (no branching, no diffs) — it is designed
    for session artefacts and agent outputs, not source code.

    Example::

        store = VersionedJSONStore("outputs/scenes/room_001")
        store.commit({"objects": [...], "style": "modern"})
        store.commit({"objects": [...], "style": "scandinavian"})  # v2
        data = store.load()          # HEAD → v2
        data = store.load(version=1) # explicit v1
        history = store.history()    # [{version, timestamp, size_bytes}, ...]
    """

    _VERSION_FMT = "v{:04d}.json"
    _HEAD_FILE = "HEAD"

    def __init__(self, root: str | Path) -> None:
        self._root = Path(root)
        self._root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal Helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _head(self) -> int:
        head_file = self._root / self._HEAD_FILE
        if not head_file.exists():
            return 0
        return int(head_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip())

    def _set_head(self, version: int) -> None:
        (self._root / self._HEAD_FILE).write_text(str(version), encoding="utf-8")

    def _version_path(self, version: int) -> Path:
        return self._root / self._VERSION_FMT.format(version)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public API
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def commit(self, data: Any) -> int:
        """Write *data* as the next version and advance HEAD.

        Args:
            data: JSON-serialisable object.

        Returns:
            New version number (1-indexed).
        """
        next_version = self._head() + 1
        envelope = {
            "_version": next_version,
            "_committed_at": datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
            "data": data,
        }
        save_json(envelope, self._version_path(next_version))
        self._set_head(next_version)
        logger.info("VersionedJSONStore committed v%04d → %s", next_version, self._root)
        return next_version

    def load(self, version: int | None = None) -> Any:
        """Load a version's data payload.

        Args:
            version: Specific version number, or None for HEAD.

        Returns:
            The ``"data"`` field of the stored envelope.

        Raises:
            FileNotFoundError: If requested version does not exist.
            ValueError:        If the store is empty (no commits yet).
        """
        v = version if version is not None else self._head()
        if v == 0:
            raise ValueError(f"Store at '{self._root}' has no commits yet.")
        path = self._version_path(v)
        if not path.exists():
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"Version v{v:04d} not found in {self._root}")
        return load_json(path)["data"]

    def history(self) -> list[dict]:
        """Return a list of version metadata dicts, newest first.

        Each dict contains: ``version``, ``committed_at``, ``size_bytes``,
        ``checksum``.
        """
        records = []
        head = self._head()
        for v in range(head, 0, -1):
            path = self._version_path(v)
            if not path.exists():
                continue
            envelope = load_json(path)
            records.append({
                "version":      v,
                "committed_at": envelope.get("_committed_at", "unknown"),
                "size_bytes":   path.stat().st_size,
                "checksum":     compute_checksum(path),
            })
        return records

    def rollback(self, version: int) -> Any:
        """Re-set HEAD to *version* (does not delete newer versions).

        Returns:
            The data at the rolled-back version.
        """
        head = self._head()
        if version < 1 or version > head:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Invalid rollback target v{version}. "
                f"Store has versions 1–{head}."
            )
        self._set_head(version)
        logger.warning(
            "VersionedJSONStore rolled back from v%04d → v%04d in %s",
            head, version, self._root,
        )
        return self.load(version)

    def latest_version(self) -> int:
        """Return the current HEAD version number (0 if no commits)."""
        return self._head()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Convenience Exports
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

__all__ = [
    # Path helpers
    "resolve_output_path",
    "ensure_output_dirs",
    "directory_size_mb",
    "clean_directory",
    # JSON I/O
    "save_json",
    "load_json",
    "load_json_safe",
    "append_json_lines",
    # Checksums
    "compute_checksum",
    "verify_checksum",
    "save_json_with_checksum",
    # Versioned store
    "VersionedJSONStore",
]
```

## File: `free_space_detector.py`
Path: `file:///d:/VisionAI-PRO-AgenticAI/VisionSpace-AI/utils/free_space_detector.py`

```python
# utils/free_space_detector.py
import logging
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Tuple

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class FreeSpaceDetector:
    """Discretizes room layout into an occupancy grid and extracts free-space polygons."""

    def __init__(self, resolution_cm: int = 5) -> None:
        self.resolution = resolution_cm

    def build_occupancy_grid(
        self, room_width_cm: int, room_depth_cm: int, objects: List[Dict[str, Any]], doors: List[Dict[str, Any]] = None, windows: List[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """Creates a grid matrix where 0 is free space, 1 is occupied, 2 is wall, 3 is window, and 4 is door clearance."""
        # Convert dimensions to grid cell coordinates
        grid_w = max(1, room_width_cm // self.resolution)
        grid_d = max(1, room_depth_cm // self.resolution)

        # Initialize empty grid (0 = free)
        cells = [[0 for _ in range(grid_w)] for _ in range(grid_d)]

        # 1. Fill walls on the outer boundaries (2 = wall)
        for y in range(grid_d):
            cells[y][0] = 2
            cells[y][grid_w - 1] = 2
        for x in range(grid_w):
            cells[0][x] = 2
            cells[grid_d - 1][x] = 2

        # 2. Fill windows (3 = window)
        if windows:
            for win in windows:
                pos = win.get("position", win.get("room_position", {"x_cm": 0, "y_cm": 0}))
                dims = win.get("estimated_dimensions") or {"width_cm": 100, "depth_cm": 20}
                w_cm = dims.get("width_cm") or 100.0
                d_cm = dims.get("depth_cm") or 20.0
                px, py = float(pos.get("x_cm") or 0.0), float(pos.get("y_cm") or 0.0)
                
                min_x = max(0, int((px - w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                max_x = min(grid_w, int((px + w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                min_y = max(0, int((py - d_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                max_y = min(grid_d, int((py + d_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                for y in range(min_y, max_y):
                    for x in range(min_x, max_x):
                        cells[y][x] = 3

        # 3. Fill door clearances (4 = door clearance)
        if doors:
            for door in doors:
                pos = door.get("position", door.get("room_position", {"x_cm": 0, "y_cm": 0}))
                dims = door.get("estimated_dimensions") or {"width_cm": 90, "depth_cm": 90}
                w_cm = dims.get("width_cm") or 90.0
                d_cm = dims.get("depth_cm") or 90.0
                px, py = float(pos.get("x_cm") or 0.0), float(pos.get("y_cm") or 0.0)
                
                min_x = max(0, int((px - w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                max_x = min(grid_w, int((px + w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                min_y = max(0, int((py - d_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                max_y = min(grid_d, int((py + d_cm/2) / self.resolution))
                for y in range(min_y, max_y):
                    for x in range(min_x, max_x):
                        cells[y][x] = 4

        # 4. Fill occupied blocks from furniture dimensions (1 = occupied)
        for obj in objects:
            pos = obj.get("position", obj.get("room_position", {"x_cm": 0, "y_cm": 0}))
            dims = obj.get("estimated_dimensions") or {"width_cm": 80, "depth_cm": 80}
            
            w_cm = dims.get("width_cm") or 80.0
            d_cm = dims.get("depth_cm") or 80.0
            px, py = float(pos.get("x_cm") or 0.0), float(pos.get("y_cm") or 0.0)
            
            # Map object relative to room bounds (clamp to valid ranges)
            min_x = max(0, int((px - w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
            max_x = min(grid_w, int((px + w_cm/2) / self.resolution))
            min_y = max(0, int((py - d_cm/2) / self.resolution))
            max_y = min(grid_d, int((py + d_cm/2) / self.resolution))

            for y in range(min_y, max_y):
                for x in range(min_x, max_x):
                    if cells[y][x] == 0:
                        cells[y][x] = 1

        return {
            "grid_resolution_cm": self.resolution,
            "width": grid_w,
            "height": grid_d,
            "cells": cells
        }

    def detect_free_regions(
        self, occupancy_grid: Dict[str, Any], room_width_cm: int, room_depth_cm: int
    ) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, Any]], List[List[Dict[str, int]]]]:
        """Identifies empty regions and compiles lists of free space polygons using BFS connected components."""
        cells = occupancy_grid["cells"]
        grid_w = occupancy_grid["width"]
        grid_d = occupancy_grid["height"]

        visited = [[False for _ in range(grid_w)] for _ in range(grid_d)]
        empty_regions: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
        free_polygons: List[List[Dict[str, int]]] = []
        region_count = 1

        for y in range(grid_d):
            for x in range(grid_w):
                if cells[y][x] == 0 and not visited[y][x]:
                    # Start BFS to find the connected components
                    component = []
                    queue = [(x, y)]
                    visited[y][x] = True

                    while queue:
                        cx, cy = queue.pop(0)
                        component.append((cx, cy))
                        for dx, dy in [(-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1), (0, 1)]:
                            nx, ny = cx + dx, cy + dy
                            if 0 <= nx < grid_w and 0 <= ny < grid_d:
                                if cells[ny][nx] == 0 and not visited[ny][nx]:
                                    visited[ny][nx] = True
                                    queue.append((nx, ny))

                    # Calculate bounding box of the component
                    min_x = min(pt[0] for pt in component)
                    max_x = max(pt[0] for pt in component)
                    min_y = min(pt[1] for pt in component)
                    max_y = max(pt[1] for pt in component)

                    w_cm = (max_x - min_x + 1) * self.resolution
                    d_cm = (max_y - min_y + 1) * self.resolution

                    # Include only components larger than 50cm x 50cm
                    if w_cm >= 50 and d_cm >= 50:
                        empty_regions.append({
                            "id": f"region_{region_count:03d}",
                            "width_cm": w_cm,
                            "depth_cm": d_cm,
                            "suitable_for": ["furniture", "seating", "storage"] if w_cm >= 100 else ["desk", "decor"]
                        })

                        # Bounding box coordinates
                        free_polygons.append([
                            {"x": min_x * self.resolution, "y": min_y * self.resolution},
                            {"x": (max_x + 1) * self.resolution, "y": min_y * self.resolution},
                            {"x": (max_x + 1) * self.resolution, "y": (max_y + 1) * self.resolution},
                            {"x": min_x * self.resolution, "y": (max_y + 1) * self.resolution}
                        ])
                        region_count += 1

        # Fallback if no empty region found
        if not empty_regions:
            w_cm = min(room_width_cm, 150)
            d_cm = min(room_depth_cm, 150)
            empty_regions.append({
                "id": "region_001",
                "width_cm": w_cm,
                "depth_cm": d_cm,
                "suitable_for": ["seating", "decor"]
            })
            free_polygons.append([
                {"x": 0, "y": 0},
                {"x": w_cm, "y": 0},
                {"x": w_cm, "y": d_cm},
                {"x": 0, "y": d_cm}
            ])

        return empty_regions, free_polygons

```

## File: `geometry_utils.py`
Path: `file:///d:/VisionAI-PRO-AgenticAI/VisionSpace-AI/utils/geometry_utils.py`

```python
# utils/geometry_utils.py
"""
VisionSpace AI — Production Geometry & Spatial Utilities
=========================================================
Provides:
  - Bounding-box arithmetic (area, IoU, centre, normalise, scale)
  - Spatial relationship detection (overlap, containment, adjacency, distance)
  - Room layout analysis (zone assignment, focal-point detection, clearance checks)
  - Grid / heat-map generation for density visualisation
  - Depth-estimation helpers (ordinal depth from bounding-box heuristics)

All functions operate on plain Python dicts / lists — no heavy ML dependency
so they can run on CPU-only Kaggle kernels without GPU overhead.

BoundingBox format used throughout::

    {
        "x":      int | float,   # left edge in pixels (or normalised 0-1)
        "y":      int | float,   # top edge in pixels (or normalised 0-1)
        "width":  int | float,   # box width
        "height": int | float,   # box height
    }
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import math
from typing import Any

from utils.constants import (
    MIN_CLEARANCE_CM,
    ZONE_FURNITURE_MAP,
    FOCAL_POINT_BY_ROOM,
    ROOM_AREA_GUIDELINES_SQM,
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Type Aliases
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BBox = dict[str, float]        # {"x", "y", "width", "height"}
Point = tuple[float, float]    # (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Basic BoundingBox Arithmetic
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def calculate_area(bbox: BBox) -> float:
    """Return the pixel area of *bbox*.

    Args:
        bbox: Dict with keys ``x``, ``y``, ``width``, ``height``.

    Returns:
        Non-negative float.  Returns 0.0 for degenerate boxes.
    """
    area = float(bbox["width"]) * float(bbox["height"])
    return max(0.0, area)


def bbox_centre(bbox: BBox) -> Point:
    """Return the centre point ``(cx, cy)`` of *bbox*."""
    cx = float(bbox["x"]) + float(bbox["width"]) / 2.0
    cy = float(bbox["y"]) + float(bbox["height"]) / 2.0
    return cx, cy


def bbox_corners(bbox: BBox) -> tuple[float, float, float, float]:
    """Return ``(x1, y1, x2, y2)`` corner coordinates from a bbox dict."""
    x1 = float(bbox["x"])
    y1 = float(bbox["y"])
    x2 = x1 + float(bbox["width"])
    y2 = y1 + float(bbox["height"])
    return x1, y1, x2, y2


def normalise_bbox(bbox: BBox, image_width: int, image_height: int) -> BBox:
    """Return a copy of *bbox* with all coordinates scaled to [0, 1].

    Args:
        bbox:         Pixel-coordinate bbox.
        image_width:  Width of the reference image in pixels.
        image_height: Height of the reference image in pixels.

    Returns:
        New bbox dict with normalised float values.
    """
    return {
        "x":      float(bbox["x"])      / image_width,
        "y":      float(bbox["y"])      / image_height,
        "width":  float(bbox["width"])  / image_width,
        "height": float(bbox["height"]) / image_height,
    }


def denormalise_bbox(bbox: BBox, image_width: int, image_height: int) -> BBox:
    """Inverse of :func:`normalise_bbox` — scale [0,1] coords to pixels."""
    return {
        "x":      float(bbox["x"])      * image_width,
        "y":      float(bbox["y"])      * image_height,
        "width":  float(bbox["width"])  * image_width,
        "height": float(bbox["height"]) * image_height,
    }


def scale_bbox(bbox: BBox, scale: float) -> BBox:
    """Uniformly scale *bbox* dimensions by *scale* (anchor = top-left)."""
    return {
        "x":      float(bbox["x"])      * scale,
        "y":      float(bbox["y"])      * scale,
        "width":  float(bbox["width"])  * scale,
        "height": float(bbox["height"]) * scale,
    }


def expand_bbox(bbox: BBox, margin: float) -> BBox:
    """Expand *bbox* by *margin* pixels on all four sides (can go negative).

    Args:
        bbox:   Source bounding box.
        margin: Pixels to add (positive) or subtract (negative) on each side.

    Returns:
        Expanded bbox; width and height are clamped to ≥ 0.
    """
    return {
        "x":      float(bbox["x"])      - margin,
        "y":      float(bbox["y"])      - margin,
        "width":  max(0.0, float(bbox["width"])  + 2 * margin),
        "height": max(0.0, float(bbox["height"]) + 2 * margin),
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Intersection / IoU
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _intersection_area(a: BBox, b: BBox) -> float:
    """Return the intersection area of two bboxes (0 if no overlap)."""
    ax1, ay1, ax2, ay2 = bbox_corners(a)
    bx1, by1, bx2, by2 = bbox_corners(b)
    ix1 = max(ax1, bx1)
    iy1 = max(ay1, by1)
    ix2 = min(ax2, bx2)
    iy2 = min(ay2, by2)
    if ix2 <= ix1 or iy2 <= iy1:
        return 0.0
    return (ix2 - ix1) * (iy2 - iy1)


def intersection_over_union(a: BBox, b: BBox) -> float:
    """Compute Intersection-over-Union (IoU) between two bboxes.

    Args:
        a: First bounding box.
        b: Second bounding box.

    Returns:
        Float in [0.0, 1.0].  Returns 0.0 if union is zero.
    """
    inter = _intersection_area(a, b)
    if inter == 0.0:
        return 0.0
    union = calculate_area(a) + calculate_area(b) - inter
    return inter / union if union > 0 else 0.0


def boxes_overlap(a: BBox, b: BBox, threshold: float = 0.0) -> bool:
    """Return True if IoU(a, b) > *threshold*."""
    return intersection_over_union(a, b) > threshold


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Spatial Relationships
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def euclidean_distance(a: BBox, b: BBox) -> float:
    """Return the Euclidean distance (pixels) between the centres of *a* and *b*."""
    (ax, ay) = bbox_centre(a)
    (bx, by) = bbox_centre(b)
    return math.hypot(ax - bx, ay - by)


def manhattan_distance(a: BBox, b: BBox) -> float:
    """Return the L1 (Manhattan) distance between the centres of *a* and *b*."""
    (ax, ay) = bbox_centre(a)
    (bx, by) = bbox_centre(b)
    return abs(ax - bx) + abs(ay - by)


def is_contained(inner: BBox, outer: BBox) -> bool:
    """Return True if *inner* bbox is fully contained within *outer* bbox."""
    ix1, iy1, ix2, iy2 = bbox_corners(inner)
    ox1, oy1, ox2, oy2 = bbox_corners(outer)
    return ix1 >= ox1 and iy1 >= oy1 and ix2 <= ox2 and iy2 <= oy2


def relative_position(a: BBox, b: BBox) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Describe the spatial relationship of *a* relative to *b*.

    Returns a dict with::

        {
            "dx":        float,        # signed horizontal offset (cx_a - cx_b)
            "dy":        float,        # signed vertical offset (cy_a - cy_b)
            "distance":  float,        # Euclidean centre-to-centre distance
            "direction": str,          # compass-style: "left", "right", "above",
                                       # "below", "above-left", …, "overlapping"
            "iou":       float,
        }
    """
    (ax, ay) = bbox_centre(a)
    (bx, by) = bbox_centre(b)
    dx = ax - bx
    dy = ay - by
    dist = math.hypot(dx, dy)
    iou = intersection_over_union(a, b)

    if iou > 0.3:
        direction = "overlapping"
    else:
        h = "left" if dx < 0 else "right"
        v = "above" if dy < 0 else "below"
        if abs(dx) < 0.1 * dist:
            direction = v
        elif abs(dy) < 0.1 * dist:
            direction = h
        else:
            direction = f"{v}-{h}"

    return {
        "dx":        round(dx, 2),
        "dy":        round(dy, 2),
        "distance":  round(dist, 2),
        "direction": direction,
        "iou":       round(iou, 4),
    }


def are_adjacent(
    a: BBox,
    b: BBox,
    gap_threshold: float = 50.0,
) -> bool:
    """Return True if *a* and *b* are within *gap_threshold* pixels of each other.

    Checks the shortest edge-to-edge distance (not centre-to-centre).

    Args:
        a:             First bbox.
        b:             Second bbox.
        gap_threshold: Max pixel gap to be considered adjacent.
    """
    ax1, ay1, ax2, ay2 = bbox_corners(a)
    bx1, by1, bx2, by2 = bbox_corners(b)

    # Horizontal gap
    hgap = max(0.0, max(bx1 - ax2, ax1 - bx2))
    # Vertical gap
    vgap = max(0.0, max(by1 - ay2, ay1 - by2))

    # If both gaps are zero, boxes are overlapping → treat as adjacent
    if hgap == 0 and vgap == 0:
        return True
    if hgap == 0:
        return vgap <= gap_threshold
    if vgap == 0:
        return hgap <= gap_threshold
    # Corner-to-corner diagonal
    return math.hypot(hgap, vgap) <= gap_threshold


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Room Layout Analysis
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def estimate_room_size(
    objects: list[dict],
    image_width: int,
    image_height: int,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Estimate relative room size from the spatial spread of detected objects.

    Uses the bounding box of all objects combined as a proxy for visible area.

    Args:
        objects:      List of detected objects, each with a ``bbox`` field.
        image_width:  Image width in pixels.
        image_height: Image height in pixels.

    Returns:
        Dict with ``coverage_ratio``, ``estimated_category`` (small/medium/large),
        and ``spread_bbox``.
    """
    if not objects:
        return {
            "coverage_ratio": 0.0,
            "estimated_category": "unknown",
            "spread_bbox": None,
        }

    xs = [obj["bbox"]["x"] for obj in objects if "bbox" in obj]
    ys = [obj["bbox"]["y"] for obj in objects if "bbox" in obj]
    x2s = [obj["bbox"]["x"] + obj["bbox"]["width"]  for obj in objects if "bbox" in obj]
    y2s = [obj["bbox"]["y"] + obj["bbox"]["height"] for obj in objects if "bbox" in obj]

    if not xs:
        return {"coverage_ratio": 0.0, "estimated_category": "unknown", "spread_bbox": None}

    spread = {
        "x":      min(xs),
        "y":      min(ys),
        "width":  max(x2s) - min(xs),
        "height": max(y2s) - min(ys),
    }
    spread_area = calculate_area(spread)
    image_area = image_width * image_height
    ratio = spread_area / image_area if image_area > 0 else 0.0

    if ratio < 0.35:
        category = "small"
    elif ratio < 0.65:
        category = "medium"
    else:
        category = "large"

    return {
        "coverage_ratio":       round(ratio, 4),
        "estimated_category":   category,
        "spread_bbox":          spread,
    }


def assign_spatial_zones(
    objects: list[dict],
    image_width: int,
    image_height: int,
    room_type: str = "living_room",
) -> list[dict]:
    """Assign a spatial zone label (top-left, top-right, …, centre) to each object.

    Divides the image into a 3×3 grid and classifies objects by where their
    centre falls.

    Args:
        objects:      List of detected objects with ``bbox`` and ``label`` fields.
        image_width:  Image width in pixels.
        image_height: Image height in pixels.
        room_type:    Room category (used for zone naming hints).

    Returns:
        Copy of *objects* with an added ``spatial_zone`` field on each item.
    """
    w_third = image_width / 3
    h_third = image_height / 3

    _grid = {
        (0, 0): "top-left",     (1, 0): "top-centre",     (2, 0): "top-right",
        (0, 1): "middle-left",  (1, 1): "centre",          (2, 1): "middle-right",
        (0, 2): "bottom-left",  (1, 2): "bottom-centre",   (2, 2): "bottom-right",
    }

    annotated = []
    for obj in objects:
        obj_copy = dict(obj)
        if "bbox" in obj:
            cx, cy = bbox_centre(obj["bbox"])
            col = min(int(cx / w_third), 2)
            row = min(int(cy / h_third), 2)
            obj_copy["spatial_zone"] = _grid.get((col, row), "unknown")
        else:
            obj_copy["spatial_zone"] = "unknown"
        annotated.append(obj_copy)

    return annotated


def detect_focal_point(
    objects: list[dict],
    room_type: str = "living_room",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Identify the likely focal point of a room from detected objects.

    Focal points are matched against the ``FOCAL_POINT_BY_ROOM`` registry,
    falling back to the largest object by area.

    Args:
        objects:   Detected object list (each has ``label`` and ``bbox``).
        room_type: Room category key.

    Returns:
        Dict with ``label``, ``bbox``, ``confidence`` (heuristic 0–1),
        ``method`` (``"registry_match"`` or ``"largest_object"``).
    """
    focal_candidates = [
        fp.replace("_", " ") for fp in FOCAL_POINT_BY_ROOM.get(room_type, [])
    ]

    for obj in objects:
        label = obj.get("label", "").lower().replace("_", " ")
        for fp in focal_candidates:
            if fp in label or label in fp:
                return {
                    "label":      obj.get("label"),
                    "bbox":       obj.get("bbox"),
                    "confidence": 0.85,
                    "method":     "registry_match",
                }

    # Fallback: largest visible object
    if objects:
        largest = max(
            (o for o in objects if "bbox" in o),
            key=lambda o: calculate_area(o["bbox"]),
            default=None,
        )
        if largest:
            return {
                "label":      largest.get("label"),
                "bbox":       largest.get("bbox"),
                "confidence": 0.55,
                "method":     "largest_object",
            }

    return {"label": None, "bbox": None, "confidence": 0.0, "method": "none"}


def check_clearances(
    objects: list[dict],
    pixels_per_cm: float = 3.0,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Check whether pairs of objects satisfy minimum clearance requirements.

    Args:
        objects:        List of objects with ``label`` and ``bbox``.
        pixels_per_cm:  Scale factor from cm to pixels (default 3 px/cm).

    Returns:
        List of violation dicts::

            {
                "object_a": str,
                "object_b": str,
                "rule":     str,
                "required_cm": float,
                "actual_cm":   float,
                "violation":   bool,
            }
    """
    violations = []

    # Only check rules we can infer from label matching
    rule_pairs = [
        ("sofa",    "coffee_table", "sofa_to_coffee"),
        ("sofa",    "tv",           "sofa_to_tv"),
        ("bed",     "wall",         "bed_sides"),
        ("chair",   "table",        "dining_chair_pull"),
    ]

    label_map = {obj.get("label", "").lower(): obj for obj in objects}

    for kw_a, kw_b, rule_key in rule_pairs:
        required_cm = MIN_CLEARANCE_CM.get(rule_key, 0)
        for label_a, obj_a in label_map.items():
            if kw_a not in label_a:
                continue
            for label_b, obj_b in label_map.items():
                if kw_b not in label_b:
                    continue
                dist_px = euclidean_distance(obj_a["bbox"], obj_b["bbox"])
                dist_cm = dist_px / pixels_per_cm
                violations.append({
                    "object_a":    label_a,
                    "object_b":    label_b,
                    "rule":        rule_key,
                    "required_cm": required_cm,
                    "actual_cm":   round(dist_cm, 1),
                    "violation":   dist_cm < required_cm,
                })

    return violations


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Density Heat-Map Grid
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_density_grid(
    objects: list[dict],
    image_width: int,
    image_height: int,
    grid_cols: int = 8,
    grid_rows: int = 8,
) -> list[list[int]]:
    """Build a 2-D density grid counting objects per cell.

    Args:
        objects:      List of objects with ``bbox`` fields.
        image_width:  Image width in pixels.
        image_height: Image height in pixels.
        grid_cols:    Number of horizontal cells.
        grid_rows:    Number of vertical cells.

    Returns:
        2-D list ``grid[row][col]`` with object counts per cell.
    """
    grid: list[list[int]] = [[0] * grid_cols for _ in range(grid_rows)]

    cell_w = image_width / grid_cols
    cell_h = image_height / grid_rows

    for obj in objects:
        if "bbox" not in obj:
            continue
        cx, cy = bbox_centre(obj["bbox"])
        col = min(int(cx / cell_w), grid_cols - 1)
        row = min(int(cy / cell_h), grid_rows - 1)
        grid[row][col] += 1

    return grid


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Depth Estimation Heuristics
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def estimate_depth_order(
    objects: list[dict],
    image_height: int,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Estimate ordinal depth (closer/farther) using vertical position heuristics.

    Objects lower in the frame (larger ``y + height``) are typically closer
    in perspective photography.  Returns *objects* sorted nearest-first with
    a ``depth_rank`` field added (1 = closest).

    Args:
        objects:      List of objects with ``bbox`` fields.
        image_height: Image height in pixels.

    Returns:
        Sorted list of objects with ``depth_rank`` (int) and
        ``depth_score`` (float 0–1, higher = nearer) added.
    """
    scored = []
    for obj in objects:
        if "bbox" not in obj:
            scored.append({**obj, "depth_rank": 999, "depth_score": 0.0})
            continue
        _, _, _, y2 = bbox_corners(obj["bbox"])
        score = y2 / image_height   # 0 (top) → 1 (bottom = nearer)
        scored.append({**obj, "depth_score": round(score, 4)})

    scored.sort(key=lambda o: o.get("depth_score", 0), reverse=True)
    for rank, obj in enumerate(scored, start=1):
        obj["depth_rank"] = rank

    return scored


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Exports
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

__all__ = [
    # Arithmetic
    "calculate_area",
    "bbox_centre",
    "bbox_corners",
    "normalise_bbox",
    "denormalise_bbox",
    "scale_bbox",
    "expand_bbox",
    # IoU / Overlap
    "intersection_over_union",
    "boxes_overlap",
    # Spatial relations
    "euclidean_distance",
    "manhattan_distance",
    "is_contained",
    "relative_position",
    "are_adjacent",
    # Room analysis
    "estimate_room_size",
    "assign_spatial_zones",
    "detect_focal_point",
    "check_clearances",
    # Utilities
    "build_density_grid",
    "estimate_depth_order",
]

```

## File: `image_preprocessing.py`
Path: `file:///d:/VisionAI-PRO-AgenticAI/VisionSpace-AI/utils/image_preprocessing.py`

```python
"""Image Preprocessing Module for VisionSpace AI.

Handles image loading, URL downloading with concurrent threads, image validation,
EXIF transposition, context safety bounds, and image count capping.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import io
import logging
import pathlib
import time
import threading
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Union

import requests
from PIL import Image, ImageOps

try:
    import pillow_heif
    pillow_heif.registe
    r_heif_opener()
    logging.getLogger("VisionSpaceAI.ImagePreprocessing").info("pillow_heif registered successfully.")
except ImportError:
    logging.getLogger("VisionSpaceAI.ImagePreprocessing").debug("pillow_heif not available. HEIC support may be limited.")

import config

logger = logging.getLogger("VisionSpaceAI.ImagePreprocessing")

# Safe configuration settings resolution
MAX_IMAGE_SIZE = getattr(config, "MAX_IMAGE_SIZE", 1024)
MAX_IMAGES = getattr(config, "MAX_IMAGES", 8)


class VisionImagePreprocessor:
    """Class responsible for fetching, validating, and preprocessing images for the vision model."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        """Initializes the VisionImagePreprocessor."""
        self.last_resize_time = 0.0
        logger.info("VisionImagePreprocessor initialized with MAX_IMAGE_SIZE=%d, MAX_IMAGES=%d", MAX_IMAGE_SIZE, MAX_IMAGES)


    @staticmethod
    def _is_url(path: str) -> bool:
        """Checks if a string is an HTTP/HTTPS URL.

        Args:
            path: String path to check.

        Returns:
            True if it starts with http:// or https://.
        """
        return path.startswith("http://") or path.startswith("https://")

    @staticmethod
    def _download_image(url: str) -> Image.Image:
        """Downloads an image from a URL with retries.

        Args:
            url: The HTTP/HTTPS URL of the image.

        Returns:
            A PIL Image.
        """
        logger.info("Downloading image from URL: %s", url)
        # Retry up to 3 times before failing
        for attempt in range(3):
            try:
                response = requests.get(url, timeout=30.0)
                response.raise_for_status()
                return Image.open(io.BytesIO(response.content))
            except Exception as e:
                logger.warning("Download attempt %d failed for %s: %s", attempt + 1, url, str(e))
                if attempt == 2:
                    raise e
                time.sleep(1.0)
        raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to download image from {url}")

    @staticmethod
    def _load_local_image(path: Union[str, pathlib.Path]) -> Image.Image:
        """Loads a local image file.

        Args:
            path: The local filesystem path.

        Returns:
            A PIL Image.
        """
        p = pathlib.Path(path)
        if not p.exists():
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"Local image not found: {p}")
        return Image.open(p)

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_image(image: Image.Image) -> Image.Image:
        """Validates that an image object is correct, has positive dimensions, and converts to RGB.

        Handles transparent modes (RGBA, LA, P) via alpha compositing and other modes appropriately.

        Args:
            image: The PIL Image to validate.

        Returns:
            A valid RGB PIL Image.
        """
        if image is None:
            raise ValueError("Image object is None.")
        if not isinstance(image, Image.Image):
            raise TypeError("Object is not a PIL Image instance.")
        if image.width <= 0 or image.height <= 0:
            raise ValueError(f"Invalid image dimensions: {image.width}x{image.height}. Dimensions must be positive.")

        # Handle conversions and transparent images nicely
        if image.mode in ("RGBA", "LA", "P"):
            logger.debug("Converting transparent image mode %s to RGB via alpha composite", image.mode)
            image = image.convert("RGBA")
            background = Image.new("RGBA", image.size, (255, 255, 255, 255))
            image = Image.alpha_composite(background, image).convert("RGB")
        elif image.mode != "RGB":
            logger.debug("Converting image mode %s to RGB", image.mode)
            image = image.convert("RGB")

        return image

    def _resize_image(self, image: Image.Image) -> Image.Image:
        """Resizes an image if its largest side exceeds MAX_IMAGE_SIZE.

        Args:
            image: PIL Image.

        Returns:
            Resized or original PIL Image.
        """
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        width, height = image.size
        largest_side = max(width, height)
        if largest_side > MAX_IMAGE_SIZE:
            ratio = MAX_IMAGE_SIZE / largest_side
            new_width = int(width * ratio)
            new_height = int(height * ratio)
            logger.info(
                "Resizing oversized image from %dx%d to %dx%d (MAX_IMAGE_SIZE=%d)",
                width, height, new_width, new_height, MAX_IMAGE_SIZE
            )
            res = image.resize((new_width, new_height), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)
            self.last_resize_time += time.perf_counter() - t0
            return res
        self.last_resize_time += time.perf_counter() - t0
        return image


    def _prepare_single_image(self, image: Union[str, pathlib.Path, Image.Image]) -> Image.Image:
        """Loads, validates, and resizes a single image input, correcting orientation with EXIF.

        Args:
            image: A PIL Image, path to a local file, or a URL string.

        Returns:
            Preprocessed RGB PIL Image.
        """
        if isinstance(image, Image.Image):
            img = image
        elif isinstance(image, pathlib.Path):
            img = self._load_local_image(image)
        elif isinstance(image, str):
            img = self._download_image(image) if self._is_url(image) else self._load_local_image(image)
        else:
            raise TypeError(f"Unsupported image input type: {type(image)}")

        # Clean image orientation using EXIF transposition before validation/resizing
        try:
            img = ImageOps.exif_transpose(img)
        except Exception as exif_err:
            logger.warning("EXIF transpose failed: %s", str(exif_err))

        return self._resize_image(self._validate_image(img))

    def _download_images_parallel(self, urls: List[str]) -> List[Image.Image]:
        """Downloads multiple image URLs concurrently using parallel threads.

        Args:
            urls: List of image URLs to download.

        Returns:
            List of downloaded PIL Images.
        """
        results: List[Optional[Image.Image]] = [None] * len(urls)
        errors: List[Optional[Exception]] = [None] * len(urls)

        def worker(idx: int, url: str) -> None:
            try:
                results[idx] = self._download_image(url)
            except Exception as e:
                errors[idx] = e

        threads = []
        for idx, url in enumerate(urls):
            t = threading.Thread(target=worker, args=(idx, url))
            t.start()
            threads.append(t)

        for t in threads:
            t.join()

        for err in errors:
            if err is not None:
                raise err

        return [r for r in results if r is not None]

    def _manage_context_size(self, images: List[Image.Image]) -> List[Image.Image]:
        """Estimates cumulative pixels and resizes images if they exceed safe VRAM budgets.

        Args:
            images: List of PIL Images.

        Returns:
            List of preprocessed and potentially resized PIL Images.
        """
        if not images:
            return images

        total_pixels = sum(img.width * img.height for img in images)
        pixel_budget = MAX_IMAGE_SIZE * MAX_IMAGE_SIZE * 5

        if total_pixels > pixel_budget:
            t0 = time.perf_counter()
            logger.info(
                "Context protection activated: Cumulative pixels %d exceeds budget %d. Resizing inputs.",
                total_pixels, pixel_budget
            )
            ratio = (pixel_budget / total_pixels) ** 0.5
            resized_images = []
            for img in images:
                new_w = max(16, int(img.width * ratio))
                new_h = max(16, int(img.height * ratio))
                resized_images.append(img.resize((new_w, new_h), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS))
            self.last_resize_time += time.perf_counter() - t0
            return resized_images

        return images

    def prepare_images(
        self,
        images: Union[Image.Image, str, pathlib.Path, List[Union[Image.Image, str, pathlib.Path]]],
    ) -> List[Image.Image]:
        """Converts diverse image inputs into preprocessed RGB images, using parallel async downloads.

        Enforces image cap limits.

        Args:
            images: A single image or a list of mixed image inputs.

        Returns:
            List of preprocessed PIL Images.
        """
        self.last_resize_time = 0.0
        if images is None:
            return []

        raw_list = images if isinstance(images, list) else [images]


        # Automatic Image Count Limit Capping
        if len(raw_list) > MAX_IMAGES:
            logger.warning(
                "Uploaded image count (%d) exceeds limit (%d). Sampling the first %d images.",
                len(raw_list), MAX_IMAGES, MAX_IMAGES
            )
            raw_list = raw_list[:MAX_IMAGES]

        urls = []
        url_positions = []
        others: List[Any] = [None] * len(raw_list)

        for idx, img in enumerate(raw_list):
            if isinstance(img, str) and self._is_url(img):
                urls.append(img)
                url_positions.append(idx)
            else:
                others[idx] = img

        # Asynchronously download URLs
        downloaded = []
        if urls:
            downloaded = self._download_images_parallel(urls)

        # Merge results
        for idx, img in zip(url_positions, downloaded):
            others[idx] = img

        processed_images = []
        for index, img in enumerate(others):
            try:
                processed_images.append(self._prepare_single_image(img))
            except Exception as e:
                logger.error("Failed to process image at index %d: %s", index, str(e))
                raise e

        # VRAM Context Protection resizing
        return self._manage_context_size(processed_images)

    def get_image_info(self, images: List[Image.Image]) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
        """Extracts metadata dimensions and mode information from a list of images.

        Args:
            images: List of PIL Images.

        Returns:
            List of dictionary metadata per image.
        """
        return [
            {"index": idx, "width": img.width, "height": img.height, "mode": img.mode}
            for idx, img in enumerate(images)
        ]

```

```python
"""JSON Parser and Validation Schemas Module for VisionSpace AI.

Defines Pydantic schemas for the agentic workflow to ensure structured validation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Type

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

logger = logging.getLogger("VisionSpaceAI.JsonParser")


# =========================================================================
# Nested Schema Helper Definitions
# =========================================================================

class SpatialObject(BaseModel):
    """Details of a single detected object in space."""
    name: str = Field(description="Name or label of the object.")
    bounding_box: List[float] = Field(
        default=[],
        description="Bounding box coordinates, typically normalized [ymin, xmin, ymax, xmax]."
    )
    confidence: float = Field(default=1.0, description="Detection confidence score from 0 to 1.")
    size_estimate: Dict[str, float] = Field(
        default={},
        description="Estimated dimensions of the object (e.g. width, height, depth in meters)."
    )


class BudgetItem(BaseModel):
    """A single furniture/materials item in the budget estimator."""
    name: str = Field(description="Name or description of the product/material.")
    estimated_price: float = Field(description="Estimated price of the item.")
    priority: str = Field(
        default="medium",
        description="Priority of purchase, e.g. 'essential', 'recommended', 'optional'."
    )


class ProductMatch(BaseModel):
    """An e-commerce product matching a recommendation."""
    item_name: str = Field(description="E-commerce catalog item name.")
    store_url: str = Field(description="URL to the store product page.")
    price: float = Field(description="Catalog item price.")
    match_score: float = Field(default=1.0, description="Similarity match score from 0 to 1.")


# =========================================================================
# Core Schemas for the 6 Agents
# =========================================================================

class VisionSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Vision Agent analyzing the room image."""
    room_type: str = Field(description="Detected room type (e.g. living room, bedroom, kitchen).")
    style: str = Field(description="Design style detected (e.g. minimalist, scandinavian, industrial).")
    lighting: str = Field(description="Lighting conditions and sources detected (e.g. bright natural, dim warm).")
    colors: List[str] = Field(description="Primary color palette detected in the space.")
    description: str = Field(description="Detailed visual analysis summary of the room.")


class SpatialSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Spatial Agent identifying objects and layout coordinates."""
    objects: List[SpatialObject] = Field(default=[], description="List of detected spatial objects.")
    dimensions_estimate: Dict[str, float] = Field(
        default={},
        description="Overall estimated room dimensions (e.g., width, length, ceiling_height in meters)."
    )
    spatial_relations: List[str] = Field(
        default=[],
        description="Descriptions of object relations (e.g., 'sofa is against the east wall')."
    )


class DesignSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Design Agent generating improvement concepts."""
    style_recommendations: List[str] = Field(description="Style upgrades or thematic recommendation list.")
    layout_changes: List[str] = Field(description="Suggested spatial reorganizations or furniture shifts.")
    furniture_additions: List[str] = Field(description="Suggested furniture additions or replacements.")
    color_palette_suggestions: List[str] = Field(description="Proposed new color palette scheme.")
    overall_concept: str = Field(description="Overall conceptual theme description.")


class BudgetSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Budget Agent listing cost estimates."""
    items: List[BudgetItem] = Field(default=[], description="Itemized budget breakdown.")
    total_estimated_cost: float = Field(description="Total summed cost of recommended design upgrades.")
    budget_status: str = Field(
        description="Statement on whether the plan fits target budget parameters (e.g., 'within budget', 'over budget')."
    )


class CommerceSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Commerce Agent sourcing matching items."""
    product_matches: List[ProductMatch] = Field(default=[], description="Sourced products from retail partners.")
    recommendations: List[str] = Field(description="General procurement advice or shopping guidance.")


class ExplainSchema(BaseModel):
    """Schema for the Explain Agent giving final justification explanations."""
    design_decisions_explanation: str = Field(description="Deep dive explanation of why design choices were made.")
    budget_justification: str = Field(description="Justification of the budget distribution.")
    next_steps: List[str] = Field(description="Recommended sequential action items for implementation.")


# =========================================================================
# Validation Helper Functions
# =========================================================================

def validate_json_schema(data: Dict[str, Any], schema: Type[BaseModel]) -> bool:
    """Validates a dictionary data structure against a given Pydantic schema class.

    Args:
        data: The JSON dictionary object to validate.
        schema: A Pydantic BaseModel class definition.

    Returns:
        True if validation succeeds, False otherwise.
    """
    if schema is None:
        return True
    try:
        if hasattr(schema, "model_validate"):
            # Pydantic V2
            schema.model_validate(data)
        elif hasattr(schema, "validate"):
            # Pydantic V1 validation helper
            schema.validate(data)
        else:
            # Direct instantiation fallback
            schema(**data)
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning("JSON Schema validation failed for %s: %s", schema.__name__, str(e))
        return False


def extract_and_validate_json(text: str) -> Optional[Any]:
    """Extracts JSON from text, parsing markdown code blocks if necessary, and validating it."""
    import re
    text = text.strip()
    
    # Try parsing directly first
    try:
        return json.loads(text)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        pass

    # Try matching ```json ... ``` code blocks
    json_block_pattern = r"```(?:json)?\s*(\{.*?\}|\[.*?\])\s*```"
    match = re.search(json_block_pattern, text, re.DOTALL)
    if match:
        try:
            return json.loads(match.group(1))
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            pass

    # Try matching any curly braces block { ... } or [ ... ]
    brace_pattern = r"(\{.*?\}|\[.*?\])"
    matches = re.findall(brace_pattern, text, re.DOTALL)
    for m in matches:
        try:
            return json.loads(m)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            pass

    return None


def has_empty_array(data: Any) -> bool:
    """Recursively checks if any array/list in the JSON structure is empty."""
    if isinstance(data, list):
        if not data:
            return True
        return any(has_empty_array(item) for item in data)
    elif isinstance(data, dict):
        return any(has_empty_array(val) for val in data.values())
    return False


def has_duplicate_objects(data: Any) -> bool:
    """Recursively checks if any array/list in the JSON contains duplicate items/objects."""
    if isinstance(data, list):
        seen = []
        for item in data:
            if isinstance(item, (dict, list)):
                serialized = json.dumps(item, sort_keys=True)
                if serialized in seen:
                    return True
                seen.append(serialized)
            else:
                if item in seen:
                    return True
                seen.append(item)
        return any(has_duplicate_objects(item) for item in data)
    elif isinstance(data, dict):
        return any(has_duplicate_objects(val) for val in data.values())
    return False


def validate_response_rules(
    response_text: str,
    parsed_json: Optional[Any],
    validator: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]
) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    """Validates the response text or parsed JSON against rule configurations.

    Args:
        response_text: The raw generated string response.
        parsed_json: The parsed JSON object (if JSON format requested).
        validator: A dictionary of validation rules.

    Returns:
        A tuple of (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if not validator:
        return True, ""

    # 1. Word count validation
    min_words = validator.get("min_words", 0)
    if min_words > 0:
        word_count = len(response_text.split())
        if word_count < min_words:
            return False, f"Response word count ({word_count}) is less than minimum words required ({min_words})."

    # If the validator has JSON-related rules but parsing failed
    requires_json = any(k in validator for k in ["required_keys", "schema", "allow_empty_arrays", "allow_duplicate_objects"])
    if requires_json and parsed_json is None:
        return False, "Response is not valid JSON, but JSON validation rules are specified."

    if parsed_json is not None:
        # 2. Required keys check
        if isinstance(parsed_json, dict):
            required_keys = validator.get("required_keys", [])
            for key in required_keys:
                if key not in parsed_json:
                    return False, f"Missing required JSON key: '{key}'."

        # 3. Schema validation (Pydantic BaseModel)
        schema_class = validator.get("schema")
        if schema_class:
            if isinstance(parsed_json, dict):
                if not validate_json_schema(parsed_json, schema_class):
                    return False, f"JSON fails validation against Pydantic schema '{schema_class.__name__}'."
            else:
                return False, f"Expected JSON object for schema validation, but got {type(parsed_json).__name__}."

        # 4. Empty arrays check
        if not validator.get("allow_empty_arrays", True):
            if has_empty_array(parsed_json):
                return False, "Response contains an empty list/array, which is forbidden."

        # 5. Duplicate objects check
        if not validator.get("allow_duplicate_objects", True):
            if has_duplicate_objects(parsed_json):
                return False, "Response contains duplicate items/objects in a list, which is forbidden."

    return True, ""

```

## File: `llm_factory.py`

```python
# utils/llm_factory.py
"""
VisionSpace AI — Production LLM Factory
=========================================
Model-agnostic factory for creating model instances and provider
configurations. Acts as a thin shim between application code and
ModelManager, keeping the rest of the codebase decoupled from
implementation details.

Responsibilities:
  - Validate and resolve task → model config from MODEL_REGISTRY
  - Build provider-specific loading kwargs (quantization, device, dtype)
  - Expose helper utilities: ModelFactory, ModelConfig, build_messages
  - Forward all inference to ModelManager (single point of truth)
  - Support multi-backend future extensibility (HuggingFace, OpenAI, vLLM)

Usage::

    from utils.llm_factory import ModelFactory

    factory = ModelFactory()
    response = factory.generate(
        task="vision",
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Describe this room."}],
        images=[pil_image],
    )
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any, Callable

import torch
from transformers import AutoModelForImageTextToText, AutoProcessor

import config
from utils.constants import TASK_KEYS

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Model Registry
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

MODEL_REGISTRY: dict[str, dict] = {
    "vision": {
        "model":     getattr(config, "MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct"),
        "loader":    AutoModelForImageTextToText,
        "processor": AutoProcessor,
        "task_type": "vision",
        "modalities": ["text", "image"],
        "max_context": 32768,
    },
    "reasoning": {
        "model":     getattr(config, "MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct"),
        "loader":    AutoModelForImageTextToText,
        "processor": AutoProcessor,
        "task_type": "text",
        "modalities": ["text"],
        "max_context": 32768,
    },
}

# Task aliases: maps agent task names to registry keys
TASK_ALIAS: dict[str, str] = {
    "vision":        "vision",
    "spatial":       "reasoning",
    "design":        "reasoning",
    "commerce":      "reasoning",
    "budget":        "reasoning",
    "explain":       "reasoning",
    "visualization": "vision",   # future: dedicated inpainting model
    "text":          "reasoning",
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data Classes
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class ModelProfile(str, Enum):
    """Speed/accuracy trade-off profile."""
    FAST     = "Fast"
    BALANCED = "Balanced"
    ACCURATE = "Accurate"


class Backend(str, Enum):
    """Inference backend selector."""
    HUGGINGFACE = "huggingface"
    OPENAI      = "openai"
    VLLM        = "vllm"


@dataclass
class ModelConfig:
    """Resolved configuration for a single model task.

    Attributes:
        task:          Logical task name (e.g. "vision", "reasoning").
        model_id:      Hugging Face model ID string.
        loader:        Model class (e.g. AutoModelForImageTextToText).
        processor:     Processor class (e.g. AutoProcessor).
        device:        Torch device string.
        dtype:         Torch dtype.
        quantization:  Quantization strategy ("AUTO", "4bit", "8bit", "none").
        profile:       Speed/accuracy profile.
        max_context:   Maximum token context window.
        modalities:    Supported input modalities.
        extra_kwargs:  Additional loader kwargs.
    """
    task:          str
    model_id:      str
    loader:        Any
    processor:     Any
    device:        str                    = field(default_factory=lambda: config.DEVICE)
    dtype:         torch.dtype            = field(default_factory=lambda: config.DTYPE)
    quantization:  str                    = field(default_factory=lambda: config.QUANTIZATION)
    profile:       ModelProfile           = ModelProfile.BALANCED
    max_context:   int                    = 32768
    modalities:    list[str]              = field(default_factory=lambda: ["text"])
    extra_kwargs:  dict[str, Any]         = field(default_factory=dict)

    def to_loader_kwargs(self) -> dict:
        """Build kwargs dict suitable for passing to the model loader."""
        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {
            "cache_dir":       config.HF_CACHE_DIR,
            "revision":        config.HF_REVISION,
            "trust_remote_code": True,
        }

        # Dtype
        if self.device == "cuda":
            kwargs["torch_dtype"] = self.dtype
        else:
            kwargs["torch_dtype"] = torch.float32

        # Quantization
        if self.quantization in ("4bit", "AUTO") and self.device == "cuda":
            try:
                from transformers import BitsAndBytesConfig
                kwargs["quantization_config"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(
                    load_in_4bit=True,
                    bnb_4bit_compute_dtype=self.dtype,
                    bnb_4bit_use_double_quant=True,
                    bnb_4bit_quant_type="nf4",
                )
            except ImportError:
                logger.warning("BitsAndBytes not available — skipping 4-bit quantization.")
        elif self.quantization == "8bit" and self.device == "cuda":
            try:
                from transformers import BitsAndBytesConfig
                kwargs["quantization_config"] = BitsAndBytesConfig(load_in_8bit=True)
            except ImportError:
                logger.warning("BitsAndBytes not available — skipping 8-bit quantization.")

        # CPU offloading for low-VRAM scenarios
        if self.device == "cuda":
            kwargs["device_map"] = "auto"
        else:
            kwargs["device_map"] = "cpu"

        kwargs.update(self.extra_kwargs)
        return kwargs

    def to_processor_kwargs(self) -> dict:
        """Build kwargs dict suitable for the processor loader."""
        return {
            "cache_dir":         config.HF_CACHE_DIR,
            "revision":          config.HF_REVISION,
            "trust_remote_code": True,
        }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ModelFactory
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class ModelFactory:
    """Model-agnostic factory for VisionSpace AI model access.

    Thin wrapper around ModelManager that resolves task → ModelConfig and
    provides a clean interface for all agent code.

    All actual model loading and inference is delegated to ModelManager.

    Args:
        backend: Inference backend (only ``huggingface`` is supported now).
    """

    def __init__(self, backend: Backend = Backend.HUGGINGFACE) -> None:
        self._backend = backend
        self._manager = None   # Lazy import

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Config Resolution
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def resolve_config(
        task: str,
        profile: ModelProfile = ModelProfile.BALANCED,
    ) -> ModelConfig:
        """Resolve a task name to a :class:`ModelConfig`.

        Args:
            task:    Logical task name (vision, reasoning, spatial, …).
            profile: Speed/accuracy profile.

        Returns:
            Populated :class:`ModelConfig` instance.

        Raises:
            ValueError: If *task* cannot be resolved.
        """
        registry_key = TASK_ALIAS.get(task.lower())
        if registry_key is None:
            available = list(TASK_ALIAS.keys())
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unknown task '{task}'. Available: {available}"
            )

        registry_entry = MODEL_REGISTRY.get(registry_key)
        if registry_entry is None:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Registry key '{registry_key}' not found in MODEL_REGISTRY."
            )

        return ModelConfig(
            task=registry_key,
            model_id=registry_entry["model"],
            loader=registry_entry["loader"],
            processor=registry_entry["processor"],
            profile=profile,
            max_context=registry_entry.get("max_context", 32768),
            modalities=registry_entry.get("modalities", ["text"]),
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Inference API
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def generate(
        self,
        task: str,
        messages: list[dict],
        images: list[Any] | None = None,
        stream_callback: Callable | None = None,
        request_id: str | None = None,
        **generation_kwargs,
    ) -> dict:
        """Generate a response for the given task and messages.

        Delegates to :class:`~models.model_manager.ModelManager`.

        Args:
            task:             Logical task name (vision, reasoning, …).
            messages:         Chat-format message list.
            images:           Optional PIL images (for vision tasks).
            stream_callback:  Optional ``on_token(str)`` callback for streaming.
            request_id:       Optional request ID for cancellation.
            **generation_kwargs: Passed to the model (temperature, max_new_tokens …).

        Returns:
            Dict with ``text``, ``request_id``, ``task``, ``duration_s``.
        """
        manager = self._get_manager()

        # Attach images to last user message if provided
        if images:
            messages = _inject_images(messages, images)

        return manager.generate(
            task=task,
            messages=messages,
            stream_callback=stream_callback,
            request_id=request_id,
            **generation_kwargs,
        )

    async def generate_async(
        self,
        task: str,
        messages: list[dict],
        images: list[Any] | None = None,
        **generation_kwargs,
    ):
        """Async wrapper — delegates to ModelManager.generate_async()."""
        manager = self._get_manager()
        if images:
            messages = _inject_images(messages, images)
        return await manager.generate_async(task=task, messages=messages, **generation_kwargs)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cache / Model Management
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def preload(self, task: str) -> None:
        """Preload (warm up) a model for the given task.

        Args:
            task: Task name to preload.
        """
        manager = self._get_manager()
        registry_key = TASK_ALIAS.get(task.lower(), task)
        logger.info("ModelFactory: preloading model for task '%s' (key='%s')", task, registry_key)
        manager.preload(registry_key)

    def unload(self, task: str) -> None:
        """Unload the model for the given task to free memory."""
        manager = self._get_manager()
        registry_key = TASK_ALIAS.get(task.lower(), task)
        manager.unload(registry_key)

    def model_status(self) -> dict:
        """Return status report from ModelManager."""
        return self._get_manager().status_report()

    def clear_cache(self) -> None:
        """Clear the Hugging Face model cache via ModelManager."""
        manager = self._get_manager()
        if hasattr(manager, "cache_manager"):
            manager.cache_manager.clear_cache()
        else:
            logger.warning("No cache_manager attached to ModelManager.")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _get_manager(self):
        if self._manager is None:
            from models.model_manager import ModelManager
            self._manager = ModelManager.get_instance()
        return self._manager


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Message Builder Utilities
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_messages(
    system: str,
    user: str,
    history: list[dict] | None = None,
) -> list[dict]:
    """Construct a chat-format message list.

    Args:
        system:  System prompt string.
        user:    User turn content.
        history: Optional prior turns to prepend (list of role/content dicts).

    Returns:
        List of ``{"role": ..., "content": ...}`` dicts.
    """
    messages: list[dict] = []
    if system:
        messages.append({"role": "system", "content": system})
    if history:
        messages.extend(history)
    messages.append({"role": "user", "content": user})
    return messages


def build_vision_messages(
    system: str,
    user: str,
    images: list[Any],
) -> list[dict]:
    """Build a message list with inline image references for vision models.

    Images are embedded as ``{"type": "image_url", "image_url": …}`` parts
    in the user content — compatible with Qwen-VL / GPT-4V style APIs.

    Args:
        system: System prompt.
        user:   Text instruction for the vision turn.
        images: List of PIL Images or URL strings.

    Returns:
        Chat message list with image content parts.
    """
    import base64
    import io

    image_parts = []
    for img in images:
        if isinstance(img, str):
            # Assume URL
            image_parts.append({
                "type":      "image_url",
                "image_url": {"url": img},
            })
        else:
            # PIL Image → base64
            try:
                buf = io.BytesIO()
                img.save(buf, format="PNG")
                b64 = base64.b64encode(buf.getvalue()).decode()
                image_parts.append({
                    "type":      "image_url",
                    "image_url": {"url": f"data:image/png;base64,{b64}"},
                })
            except Exception as exc:
                logger.warning("Could not encode image: %s", exc)

    user_content = image_parts + [{"type": "text", "text": user}]
    return [
        {"role": "system", "content": system},
        {"role": "user",   "content": user_content},
    ]


def _inject_images(messages: list[dict], images: list[Any]) -> list[dict]:
    """Attach *images* to the last user message in *messages*.

    Converts the last user message's ``content`` from a plain string to a
    list of content parts including image placeholders recognised by
    HuggingFace vision processors.

    Args:
        messages: Existing message list.
        images:   PIL images or URL strings to attach.

    Returns:
        Updated message list (copy, original unmodified).
    """
    messages = [dict(m) for m in messages]   # shallow copy
    for i in range(len(messages) - 1, -1, -1):
        if messages[i]["role"] == "user":
            existing = messages[i]["content"]
            if isinstance(existing, str):
                messages[i]["content"] = [
                    *[{"type": "image", "image": img} for img in images],
                    {"type": "text",  "text": existing},
                ]
            elif isinstance(existing, list):
                messages[i]["content"] = [
                    *[{"type": "image", "image": img} for img in images],
                    *existing,
                ]
            break
    return messages


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-Level Singleton
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_default_factory: ModelFactory | None = None


def get_factory() -> ModelFactory:
    """Return the module-level singleton :class:`ModelFactory`."""
    global _default_factory
    if _default_factory is None:
        _default_factory = ModelFactory()
    return _default_factory


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Exports
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

__all__ = [
    "MODEL_REGISTRY",
    "TASK_ALIAS",
    "ModelProfile",
    "Backend",
    "ModelConfig",
    "ModelFactory",
    "build_messages",
    "build_vision_messages",
    "get_factory",
]
```

## File: `object_enricher.py`
